2026 – 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

Wasatch Front Regional Council

2026 – 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

Financial Plan
Tables 4.1 – 4.8 show that scheduled funds do not exceed available funds in both
Urbanized Areas during the first four fiscal program years 2026 – 2029, for the four
funding programs administered by the Wasatch Front Regional Council. In a few
instances, usually in the 5th or 6th year of the program (2030-2031 - Concept
Development), the scheduled funds may exceed the available funds by a small
percentage for project programming purposes.
Table 5.1 – 5.3 shows a summary of other federal and state funding administered by
the Utah Department of Transportation programmed during the same time period in
both urbanized areas, as well as in Morgan and Tooele counties.
Table 6.1 shows a similar summary for the federal and local funds administered by
the Utah Transit Authority.
The underlying assumptions for future available revenues include the following: For
Federal Highway Administration (FHWA) funds, levels of funding are programmed
based on the Federal Fiscal Year (FFY) 2025 apportionments in the Infrastructure
Investment and Jobs Act (IIJA), which for programming purposes, was assumed to
remain flat through the duration of the Transportation Improvement Program (TIP).
Regarding inflation adjustments, project costs for the STP, CMAQ, TAP, and CRP
programs administered by the WFRC include inflation factors from the year of
original project cost estimating. The federal aid agreements entered into by local
governments include commitment on their part to maintain the transportation facility
and equipment that is paid for with federal dollars. Programs/projects administered by
UDOT and UTA also include inflation factors each year from time of programming to
date of construction. UDOT and UTA both practice principles of not building more
facilities and equipment than they are able to maintain.

2026 – 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

2026– 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

Performance Measures
The Moving Ahead for Progress in the 21st Century Act (MAP-21),
signed into law in 2012, included several provisions that collectively are
transforming the Federal surface transportation program to be focused on
the achievement of performance outcomes.
The “Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act (IIJA), signed in 2021, built
on the MAP-21 changes and provided long-term funding certainty for
surface transportation infrastructure planning and investment.

2026 - 2031
TRANSPORTATION
IMPROVEMENT PROGRAM (TIP)
PROJECTS

For the
WASATCH FRONT REGIONAL
COUNCIL (WFRC) AREA
Wasatch Front Regional Council |

2026– 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

2026– 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

In a continuing, cooperative, and comprehensive (“3-C”) planning process, Wasatch Front Regional Council, the
Utah Department of Transportation (UDOT), Utah Transit Authority (UTA), and the local governments work
together to identify projects that will build and improve the transportation systems along the Wasatch Front.
Usually, each project that is reviewed and approved into the Transportation Improvement Program accomplishes
multiple types of improvements. Project improvements are not audited individually but holistically recognized as
the entire system improves and goals are acknowledged through reduced crashes, improved mobility, longer
lasting facilities, and improved air quality, etc. The current projects, programmed in one of the four transportation
programs (Surface Transportation Program (STP), Congestion Mitigation/ Air Quality (CMAQ), Transportation
Alternatives Program (TAP), and Carbon Reduction Program (CRP) for which WFRC is responsible to program,
have been prioritized in one of the following performance measure categories and examples of various projects are
listed on the following pages.
Performance Measure
1 Pavement Condition; Improve and Maintain Existing Pavement and Transportation Investments (Asset
Management). The WFRC’s Transportation Improvement Program (TIP) seeks to keep pavements and transit
infrastructure in good condition. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 28 projects totaling $72 million are identified to help
achieve progress in the Region’s system maintenance. These projects include roadway reconstructions, improving
drainage, and others.
2 Mobility and System Reliability; An efficient, reliable, and well-connected transportation system for moving
people and goods.
3 Operation and System Management; Maintain and improve the performance of the existing transportation
system. The WFRC’s Transportation Improvement Program (TIP) seeks to maintain and improve mobility and
reliability of the transportation system, as well as maintain and improve the performance of the existing system
through adding roadway capacity, intersection improvements, transit improvements, and active transportation
facilities across the Region. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 263 projects totaling $517 million are identified to help achieve
progress in the Region’s reliability and performance. These projects include intersection improvements, such as
turning lanes and conversions to roundabouts, installing communication devices, building access management
improvements, bus rapid transit operations, and grade-separated intersections.
4 Safety; A safe and secure transportation system. The WFRC’s Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)
seeks to improve regional roadway safety by improving roadways, intersections, and bicycle and pedestrian
facilities. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 93 projects totaling $137 million are identified to help achieve progress in the
Region’s safety. These projects include grade-separated pedestrian crossings, filling in gaps in the sidewalk
network, safe routes to school, intersection signalization, removing obstacles, and realigning intersections.
5 Environmental Sustainability; A transportation system that enhances the natural, cultural, and built environment.
6 Livability and Travel Choice; A multimodal system that provides travel options to support a livable urban
environment. The WFRC’s Transportation Improvement Program (TIP) seeks to promote environmental
sustainability, livability, and travel choice. The CMAQ and CRP programs in particular identify projects that
improve the quality of life by providing multimodal transportation flexibility, access to opportunities, and
improved air quality. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 177 projects totaling $258 million are identified to help achieve
progress in reducing the Region’s emissions and providing transportation choices. These projects include bus rapid
transit implementation and electrification, bike share expansion, new bike facilities, and a comprehensive travel
demand management program.

2026– 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)
7 Economic Vitality; A transportation system that supports economic vitality for residents and businesses.
8 Funding and Cost Effectiveness; Collaboration in funding transportation projects that maximize user benefits
9 Transit Safety Performance; Maintain and improve a safe and secure transit system. The WFRC’s Transportation
Improvement Program (TIP) seeks to maintain and improve transit system safety through appropriate system
maintenance and various service and accessibility improvements. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 10 projects totaling $23
million are identified to help achieve progress in transit system safety. These projects include replacing light rail
vehicles, increasing park and ride capacity, and various operational improvements and service expansions.
10 State of Good Repair; Improve and manage public transportation assets and increase reliability. The WFRC’s
Transportation Improvement Program (TIP) seeks to manage transit assets and increase transit system reliability
through both technological and infrastructure maintenance and improvements. In the 2026-2031 TIP, 43 projects
totaling $64 million are identified to help achieve progress in transit asset management and system reliability.
These projects include a new on-board technology system, planning for new capacity, and additional infrastructure
maintenance and improvements.

2026 – 2031 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP)

Wasatch Front Regional Council |

Box Elder County
The Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC) is responsible for coordinating the transportation planning process in the Salt Lake/
West Valley and the Ogden/ Layton urbanized areas as the designated Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO).
Only the projects within the urbanized boundaries of the Ogden/ Layton area (Brigham City, Perry, and Willard) are actually part
of the WFRC 2023-2028 Transportation Improvement Program (TIP). All other projects in the Box Elder County section of this
table are listed for reference only.

The Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC) is responsible for coordinating the transportation planning process in the Salt Lake/ West Valley and

Morgan County
Morgan County and its lone municipality joined the Regional Council in 1972. And like Tooele County, Morgan County is not within the Urbanized
Boundaries of Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC), but they are a member of the Regional Council Board and as such, their projects are included
in the WFRC TIP as a resource to the County and the City.
Morgan City is the only incorporated city in Morgan County.

Statewide
Projects that are included in programs often cross city and county
lines and as a result, frequently include projects within the
boundaries of one or more of the Metropolitan Planning
Organizations (MPO).
The “Statewide County Projects” will identify projects and
programs in each of the UDOT Regions across the state, but for
programming and information purposes, they are listed all
together.

$10,000

Tooele County
In October 2004, the Tooele Valley Rural Planning Organization (RPO) was formed to establish a process to assist the local jurisdictions within Tooele
Valley in working cooperatively to plan the transportation system and prioritize transportation projects.
Although Tooele County is not within the Urbanized Boundaries of Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC), they are a member of the Regional
Council Board and as such, their
projects are included in the
WFRC TIP as a resource to the
County and the Cities and
Towns.
The Cities and Towns of Tooele
County include;
Grantsville City
Ophir Town
Rush Valley Town
Stockton Town
Tooele City
Vernon Town
Wendover City

Various
Projects that are included in “Various Projects” often cross city and
county lines and as a result, frequently include projects within the
boundaries of one or more of the Metropolitan Planning
Organization (MPO).
The “Various Projects” will identify projects and programs in each of
the UDOT Regions across the state, but for programming and
information are listed all together.

Weber County
The Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC) is responsible for coordinating the transportation planning process in the Salt Lake/ West Valley and
the Ogden-Layton urbanized areas as the designated Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO).
The Cities of Weber County include;

Harrisville

Various County
These are listed in the 2026-2031 TIP as
programs that identify the funding source for
the programs and not the specific projects in
the program.
These programs include Bridge Inspection,
Region Project Concept Development, and
Bridge Loading, Scour Inspection, Pavement
Rehabilitation,
Pavement
Maintenance,
Transportation Solutions Program, etc.

Transit
The Federal Transit
Administration
coordinates with the
Utah Transportation
Department (UDOT),
the Utah Transit
Authority (UTA), and a
few smaller transit
agencies within the
State. UTA and UDOT
coordinate with WFRC
to program these funds
within the Wasatch
Front Region.
